Generated from the actual merged runtime registry. Rebuild with `node scripts/evaluation/buildCanonProjectionReview.js` after registry changes. Runtime availability is an implementation status, never human approval.

Human verdicts are preserved only for the exact source, projection and text hash. Unreviewed or changed rows remain pending. Review meaning fidelity, assumptions about unprovided life events, absent-stage implications, selected-deck names, and card/deck support for each visual anchor. A scene-word blacklist cannot establish semantic safety. Context-specific pair prose is not inherited from complete triads.

Enrichment review keeps full prose approval (`human_verdict`) separate from projection approval (`projection_verdict`). New records carry a stable `source_id`, source URL/method/title, `projection_text`, and `projection_version`. A blank or rejected projection verdict omits the meaning from cross-deck generation with `missing_or_unapproved_meaning_projection`; approval of an old full passage never grants projection approval. Regenerating a queue preserves reviewed rows and their existing columns exactly when no new drafts are added. General-context enrichment remains excluded.

Full authored text is retained for history and editorial comparison. Only supplied text and permitted anchors reach generation. Cross-deck references carry meaning-only text and no foreign visual anchors. Research sources are provenance for internally authored interpretation, never claimed quotations.

## 1. full: triad / death-temperance-star / general / rws-1909

Review ID: 62447daf1d4780f92062601fb3c6fd36680d97e112245b71bbc26bbbcc5c093a. Text SHA-256: db090cb4de59cf0471cb8b4c5c47e4a5894e5c1cb034af3ba920ac3cc2df7993.

Authored display/history: Death, Temperance and The Star offer a symbolic relationship between endings, integration and hope. If an ending is part of your situation, consider what needs acknowledgment and what a patient pace could look like. Small acts of care may support a steadier orientation without promising recovery or asking you to deny pain. Look to the skeleton on the white horse and banner, Temperance's angel pouring between cups with one foot in water and one on land, and the Star's large star over the pitchers by the pool.

Supplied title: The Healing Arc. Theme: Ending → Integration → Renewal.

Supplied text: Death, Temperance and The Star offer a symbolic relationship between endings, integration and hope. If an ending is part of your situation, consider what needs acknowledgment and what a patient pace could look like. Small acts of care may support a steadier orientation without promising recovery or asking you to deny pain. Look to the skeleton on the white horse and banner, Temperance's angel pouring between cups with one foot in water and one on land, and the Star's large star over the pitchers by the pool.

Supplied visual anchors: skeleton, white horse, banner, angel, cups, one foot in water, one foot on land, large star, pitchers, pool.

Authored display/history: The Devil, The Tower and The Sun can connect attachment, disruption and clarity as symbolic themes. If a limiting pattern is relevant, consider what feels restrictive, which assumptions deserve examination and what supports honest expression. Disruption is neither required nor a promise of renewed freedom. The horned figure and chained figures, the lightning-split tower with falling figures, and the radiant sun over the joyful child and sunflowers mirror the release.

Supplied title: The Liberation Arc. Theme: Bondage → Rupture → Freedom.

Supplied text: The Devil, The Tower and The Sun can connect attachment, disruption and clarity as symbolic themes. If a limiting pattern is relevant, consider what feels restrictive, which assumptions deserve examination and what supports honest expression. Disruption is neither required nor a promise of renewed freedom. The horned figure and chained figures, the lightning-split tower with falling figures, and the radiant sun over the joyful child and sunflowers mirror the release.

Supplied visual anchors: horned figure, chained figures, lightning, tower, falling figures, radiant sun with face, joyful child, sunflowers.

Disruption is neither required nor a promise of renewed freedom. The horned figure and chained figures, the lightning-split tower with falling figures, and the radiant sun over the joyful child and sunflowers mirror the release.

Supplied title: The Liberation Arc. Theme: Bondage → Rupture → Freedom.

Authored display/history: Withdraw to listen (The Hermit). Release the need to force an answer (The Hanged Man). Then move through ambiguity with gentleness—dreams, symbols, and intuition are part of the map (The Moon). This arc rewards patience over certainty. Anchor the inner work in the lantern and six-pointed star, the Hanged Man's halo on the tree, and the Moon's face between the two towers, the dog and wolf, and the winding path.

Supplied title: The Inner Work Arc. Theme: Solitude → Surrender → Mystery.

Authored display/history: When cycles shift beyond your control (Wheel of Fortune), face the reckoning with honesty (Justice), then release attachment to outcome (The Hanged Man). Acceptance is not defeat—it is wisdom that conserves energy for what you can influence. The turning wheel with the sphinx and snake, the scales and sword, and the Hanged Man's halo on the tree emphasize truth with surrender.

Supplied title: The Karmic Acceptance Arc. Theme: Fate Turns → Truth Demanded → Surrender to Flow.

Authored display/history: The Tower, The Star and The Moon can frame disruption, hope and uncertainty. If change is part of your situation, consider what is known, what remains unclear and what offers a small point of orientation. Intuition can be explored alongside evidence without promising recovery or treating uncertainty as proof. The lightning-struck tower and falling figures, the Star's large star and pitchers by the pool, and the Moon's face between the two towers with the dog and wolf keep the path grounded.

Supplied title: Disruption, Hope and Uncertainty. Theme: Upheaval → Hope Restored → Navigating Uncertainty.

Authored display/history: The Tower, The Star and The Moon can offer a relationship lens on unsettled assumptions, hope and uncertainty. If those themes fit, consider what you know, what you need to ask and which boundaries support your choices. A breakup, restored self-worth or clearer intuition is not an outcome these cards can establish.

Supplied title: Disruption, Hope and Uncertainty. Theme: Upheaval → Hope Restored → Navigating Uncertainty.

The lion and infinity symbol, the Hermit's lantern on the mountain peak, and the wheel itself mark courage, guidance, and cycles.

Supplied title: The Inner Mastery Through Solitude Arc. Theme: Taming Inner Beasts → Solitary Wisdom → Accepting Cycles.

Supplied title: . Theme: Transformation clearing into hope.

Review ID: bf3799f5dba031a69d502dc7a21e58c9f78ad09fb8075ee9b7fac68593164186. Text SHA-256: 963bcf08999a63cf63f80a0b2ae6f75d5cef29b2c69b35c76336f198ff135e16.

Matched canonical IDs: 14, 17.

Authored display/history: Death, Temperance and The Star offer a symbolic relationship between endings, integration and hope. If an ending is part of your situation, consider what needs acknowledgment and what a patient pace could look like. Small acts of care may support a steadier orientation without promising recovery or asking you to deny pain. Look to the skeleton on the white horse and banner, Temperance's angel pouring between cups with one foot in water and one on land, and the Star's large star over the pitchers by the pool.

Supplied title: Balance and possibility. Theme: Balance and possibility.

Supplied text: Patient integration and hope can reinforce each other. Try one manageable adjustment that supports balance, and give a promising possibility room to develop without demanding certainty.

Supplied visual anchors: (none).

Runtime status: available. Meaning fidelity: pending. Absent-stage review: pending. Human semantic approval: pending.

Supplied title: Fairness and perspective. Theme: Fairness and perspective.

Matched canonical IDs: 17, 18.

Authored display/history: The Tower, The Star and The Moon can frame disruption, hope and uncertainty. If change is part of your situation, consider what is known, what remains unclear and what offers a small point of orientation. Intuition can be explored alongside evidence without promising recovery or treating uncertainty as proof. The lightning-struck tower and falling figures, the Star's large star and pitchers by the pool, and the Moon's face between the two towers with the dog and wolf keep the path grounded.

Supplied title: Hope and uncertainty. Theme: Hope and uncertainty.

Supplied text: Hope and uncertainty can share the same space. Let a promising possibility guide attention while leaving room for unanswered questions, and check what you sense against what you can observe.

Supplied visual anchors: (none).

Authored display/history: The Tower, The Star and The Moon can frame disruption, hope and uncertainty. If change is part of your situation, consider what is known, what remains unclear and what offers a small point of orientation. Intuition can be explored alongside evidence without promising recovery or treating uncertainty as proof. The lightning-struck tower and falling figures, the Star's large star and pitchers by the pool, and the Moon's face between the two towers with the dog and wolf keep the path grounded.

Supplied title: Disruption and uncertainty. Theme: Disruption and uncertainty.

Supplied text: Disruption and uncertainty invite careful orientation. Notice what you know, what remains unclear, and which small action would help you feel grounded without turning an impression into a fact.
